## bac-philosophie: Baccalauréat épreuve de philosophie (terminale, voie générale)

*A non-English national exam with essay papers (France).* Score **100**, planned as **exam**, due 2027-01-12, 31.8 s, 3 call(s). Stages: Reading your week → Reading your week · 0 commitments → Planning Baccalauréat épreuve de philosophie (terminale, voie général → Finding the syllabus → Researching this syllabus for the first time → Read 8 sources → Double-checking the topics → Double-check made 14 corrections → 16 topics from research, double-checked → Building your plan → 45 sessions to Mon 11 Jan → Fitting 45 pieces of work into your free time → 141 checks passed.
- Strategy: 16 topics revised and practised in order, 5 mixed reviews, 3 timed papers (Épreuve écrite), light final day. Time is tight, so sessions are short.
- Critics: realism 100: Every day is within what you usually sustain.; exam_prep 50: fewer than two spaced retrieval sessions; no light review on Mon 11 Jan; front_loading 100: Deadline work finishes with room to spare.; interleaving 100: Practice is mixed across subjects.; fragmentation 100: Sessions are a workable length.; neglect 100: Every active goal has time.
- Daily cap: 60 min
- Syllabus **Baccalauréat, épreuve de philosophie (terminale générale)** from model (researched), library new v1 CHECKED; identity Ministère de l'Éducation nationale (France) / Baccalauréat / Philosophie / Épreuve de philosophie
- Papers: Épreuve écrite 240 min, 20 marks
- Sources: https://education.gouv.fr/bo/BoAnnexes/2000/hs8/hs8.pdf, http://philosophie.ac-besancon.fr/wp-content/uploads/sites/36/2021/09/ra21_lycee_g_1-t_hlp_philo_questions-evaluation_exercices-travaux-epreuves.pdf, https://pedagogie.ac-aix-marseille.fr/jcms/c_11236706/de/jcms/c_10696609/de/upload/docs/application/pdf/2024-03/upload/docs/application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.document/2022-11/upload/docs/application/pdf/2024-03/aide.html, https://dfglfa.net/dfg/images/DFG/Info_Dokumente/2021Lehrplaene_Philosophie/LP_II_DFG_Philosophie_Juni2016_2_Dateien_zusammengefgt.pdf, https://philosophie.ac-normandie.fr/spip.php?article640, https://pedagogie.ac-nantes.fr/medias/fichier/bo-n-21_1779956249149?ID_FICHE=164449&INLINE=FALSE

Topics:

1. **La conscience, l'inconscient, le sujet** w2 d2: Distinguer conscience immédiate, conscience réfléchie et conscience de soi; Exposer la découverte freudienne de l'inconscient et ses topiques; Problématiser l'unité du sujet entre conscience et inconscient; Analyser un texte de Descartes, Locke, Nietzsche ou Freud sur le sujet
2. **La perception et autrui** w2 d2: Distinguer sensation, perception et interprétation du donné; Comparer les analyses de la perception chez Descartes, Berkeley et Merleau-Ponty; Montrer comment autrui est un autre moi et une altérité irréductible; Expliquer le rôle du regard d'autrui (Hegel, Sartre) dans la constitution du soi
3. **Le langage** w2 d2: Distinguer signe, symbole, langage animal et langage humain; Expliquer le caractère conventionnel et arbitraire des signes; Analyser les fonctions du langage : nommer, signifier, communiquer, agir; Discuter l'idée que les limites de mon langage sont les limites de mon monde
4. **La raison et le réel : la vérité et la croyance** w3 d3: Distinguer opinion, croyance, doute et savoir; Exposer la méthode cartésienne du doute et le cogito; Expliquer la démarche expérimentale et le rôle de l'hypothèse; Discuter les critères de la vérité : cohérence, correspondance, évidence
5. **La science et l'expérience** w2 d3: Distinguer science et technique, théorie et observation; Expliquer le rôle de l'induction et sa critique par Hume; Analyser les révolutions scientifiques et la notion de paradigme (Kuhn, Bachelard); Discuter les limites de la connaissance scientifique et la notion de réfutabilité
6. **La liberté et le déterminisme** w3 d3: Distinguer libre arbitre, liberté d'action et liberté politique; Exposer le déterminisme et les objections qu'il oppose à la liberté; Analyser le rapport entre liberté et nécessité (Spinoza, Leibniz); Discuter l'idée d'une liberté construite par la connaissance de soi
7. **L'art et le beau** w2 d2: Distinguer le beau, l'agréable et le sublime (Kant); Analyser le rapport entre art, imitation et création; Expliquer la fonction de l'art : expression, connaissance, dévoilement; Discuter la question du jugement de goût et de l'universalité du beau
8. **La nature** w2 d2: Distinguer nature et culture, nature et artifice; Analyser la nature comme norme ou comme modèle (droit naturel, retour à la nature); Discuter la maîtrise technique de la nature et ses limites; Expliquer le rapport entre nature et liberté humaine
9. **Le vivant** w2 d2: Distinguer le vivant de la matière inerte et de la machine; Analyser la notion de finalité dans le vivant (Aristote, Kant); Discuter les frontières du vivant : espèce, individu, ADN, biotechnologies; Problématiser les rapports entre l'homme et l'animal
10. **Le travail et la technique** w2 d2: Distinguer travail, emploi, activité et loisir; Analyser la valeur du travail : peine, émancipation, aliénation (Hegel, Marx); Expliquer le rôle de l'outil et de la machine dans la technique; Discuter les enjeux de la technique : maîtrise, dépendance, responsabilité
11. **Le devoir et le bonheur** w2 d2: Distinguer le devoir moral et le désir de bonheur; Exposer les morales du devoir (Kant) et les morales du bonheur (eudémonisme, utilitarisme); Problématiser le conflit entre obligation et inclination; Analyser la notion de bonheur et son caractère relatif
12. **La morale, le droit et la politique ; l'État et** w3 d3: Distinguer obligation morale, contrainte juridique et légitimité; Exposer la théorie du contrat social (Hobbes, Locke, Rousseau); Analyser les formes de l'État : État de droit, État-providence, souveraineté; Discuter le rapport entre liberté individuelle et autorité politique
13. **Le temps, l'existence et la mort** w2 d3: Distinguer temps vécu, temps mesuré et temps physique; Analyser la conscience du temps (Augustin, Bergson, Heidegger); Discuter la question du sens de l'existence; Analyser le rapport de l'homme à sa mort (Épicure, Montaigne)
14. **La religion** w2 d2: Distinguer foi, croyance religieuse et savoir rationnel; Analyser les fonctions de la religion : sens, cohésion, loi morale; Exposer les critiques de la religion (Feuerbach, Marx, Nietzsche, Freud); Discuter le rapport entre religion et raison, religion et liberté de conscience
15. **La logique et les mathématiques** w2 d2: Distinguer démontrer, prouver, argumenter et convaincre; Identifier les formes valides d'inférence et les sophismes; Expliquer la différence entre vérité et validité logique; Analyser la démonstration mathématique et son rapport à l'expérience
16. **La philosophie et la métaphysique** w1 d2: Définir la philosophie et son rapport aux sciences et aux arts; Expliquer la démarche de la question philosophique et du problème; Analyser les questions métaphysiques : unité, être, cause, absolu; Situer quelques grandes démarches (Platon, Descartes, Kant, Hegel)

## fbise-hssc2-physics: FBISE HSSC-II Physics annual exam

*Pakistan federal board, intermediate (HSSC-II) physics.* Score **100**, planned as **exam**, due 2027-01-27, 23.3 s, 3 call(s). Stages: Reading your week → Reading your week · 0 commitments → Planning FBISE HSSC-II Physics annual exam → Finding the syllabus → Researching this syllabus for the first time → Read 8 sources → Double-checking the topics → Double-check made 4 corrections → 10 topics from research, double-checked → Building your plan → 92 sessions to Tue 26 Jan → Fitting 92 pieces of work into your free time → 200 checks passed.
- Strategy: 10 topics revised and practised in order, 3 mixed reviews, 5 timed Theorys, light final day.
- Critics: realism 100: Every day is within what you usually sustain.; exam_prep 50: fewer than two spaced retrieval sessions; no light review on Tue 26 Jan; front_loading 100: Deadline work finishes with room to spare.; interleaving 100: Practice is mixed across subjects.; fragmentation 100: Sessions are a workable length.; neglect 100: Every active goal has time.
- Daily cap: 120 min
- Syllabus **FBISE HSSC-II Physics (National Curriculum 2022-23)** from model (researched), library new v1 CHECKED; identity FBISE / HSSC-II / Physics
- Papers: Theory 155 min, 68 marks
- Sources: https://fbise.edu.pk/Curriculum/22-23/15_%20NCP%20Physics%20PG%209-%2012.pdf, https://mail.fbise.edu.pk/ModelPaper/2025/Practical%20Based%20Assessment%20Composite/Final%20Physics%20PBA%20HSSC%20Merged.pdf, https://fbisesolvedpastpapers.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/class-12-maths-model-paper-2.pdf, https://fbisepastpapers.com/class-12-physics-model-question-paper, https://gotest.com.pk/wp-content/uploads/2019/06/etsyllabus2018mdcatguide.com_page-0033.pdf, https://staging.studyplusplus.com/fbise/12/physics

Topics:

1. **Electrostatics** w3 d2: State Coulomb's law and apply it to systems of point charges; Define electric field and calculate field due to point charges and dipoles; Define electric flux and state Gauss's law with its applications; Derive field of a charged sphere, sheet and cylinder using Gauss's law; Define electric potential and relate potential gradient to field strength; Calculate electric potential energy of point charge systems; Define electron volt and convert between eV and joules; Describe capacitors, electric polarization and energy stored; charging and discharging
2. **Current Electricity** w3 d2: Define current, drift velocity and current density; derive I = nAve; State and apply Ohm's law; describe resistance and resistivity of conductors; Describe effect of temperature on resistance of metals and semiconductors; Derive and use the formula for combination of resistors in series and parallel; State Kirchhoff's first and second laws and apply them to circuits; Solve circuit problems using Kirchhoff's laws; Calculate electrical power and energy dissipated in circuits; Describe the potentiometer and its use in measuring emf and potential difference
3. **Electromagnetism** w3 d2: Define magnetic flux density and describe magnetic field due to a straight conductor; Describe the magnetic field of a solenoid and of a current loop; Calculate force on a current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field; Calculate force on a moving charge and describe circular motion in a magnetic field; Determine torque on a current loop and describe the moving-coil galvanometer; Describe the construction and working of an ammeter and a voltmeter; Explain the working principle of a cathode ray oscilloscope
4. **Electromagnetic Induction** w2 d2: State Faraday's law and Lenz's law and apply them to find induced emf; Relate induced emf to rate of change of flux and to motional emf; Define self-inductance and mutual inductance with their units; Calculate energy stored in an inductor; Describe the principle and working of an AC generator; Describe the principle and working of a transformer and energy losses; Explain eddy currents and their uses and drawbacks
5. **Alternating Current** w3 d2: Define alternating current and describe its waveform, peak and rms values; Calculate reactance of a capacitor and an inductor; Determine impedance of series RLC circuits and draw phasor diagrams; Explain resonance in an AC circuit and use the resonant frequency formula; Describe power in AC circuits and the power factor; Describe the components and working of an AC generator and of a transformer in AC transmission; Explain advantages of AC over DC for transmission of electric power
6. **Physics of Solids** w3 d3: Describe crystalline and amorphous solids and their properties; Describe energy bands in solids and classify conductors, insulators and semiconductors; Distinguish intrinsic and extrinsic semiconductors; describe p-type and n-type doping; Describe the p-n junction and its depletion region; Describe forward and reverse biasing of a diode and draw its characteristic curve; Describe the use of a diode as a rectifier; Describe the structure and working of an LED and a photodiode; Describe the structure of an npn transistor and the action of a transistor amplifier
7. **Electronics** w2 d3: Explain logic gates AND, OR, NOT with truth tables; Explain universal gates NAND and NOR with truth tables; Combine gates to form simple logic circuits; Describe operational amplifier as an ideal amplifier and its use; Distinguish analogue and digital signals and describe digital electronics; Describe the working of a cathode ray oscilloscope in displaying signals
8. **Dawn of Modern Physics** w3 d3: Describe the frame of reference and state Einstein's postulates of special relativity; Explain time dilation, length contraction and mass variation; Calculate mass-energy equivalence using E = mc²; Describe black body radiation and Planck's quantum hypothesis; Explain the photoelectric effect and state its laws; Use Einstein's photoelectric equation and describe the effect of intensity and frequency; Describe the Compton effect and its significance; Explain wave-particle duality and the de Broglie wavelength
9. **Atomic Spectra** w3 d2: Describe the Bohr model of the hydrogen atom and its postulates; Derive and use the expression for energy levels and radii of hydrogen; Explain the origin of line spectra: emission and absorption spectra; Describe the spectral series of hydrogen and the Rydberg formula; Relate spectral lines to energy level transitions; Describe the limitations of the Bohr model and the idea of the laser; Describe the working of a laser and its applications
10. **Nuclear Physics** w3 d3: Describe the structure of the nucleus, atomic number and mass number; Describe isotopes and the basic forces of nature; Define mass defect and calculate binding energy per nucleon; Describe radioactivity and the properties of alpha, beta and gamma radiation; State and use the radioactive decay law and half-life; Distinguish nuclear fission and nuclear fusion with examples; Describe nuclear reactors and the hazards and uses of nuclear radiation; Describe the uses of radioisotopes in medicine and industry

## cfa-level-1: CFA Level I exam

*Professional finance exam, ten official topic areas over five months.* Score **95**, planned as **exam**, due 2027-04-12, 18.5 s, 3 call(s). Stages: Reading your week → Reading your week · 0 commitments → Planning CFA Level I exam → Finding the syllabus → Researching this syllabus for the first time → Read 8 sources → Double-checking the topics → Double-check made 2 corrections → 12 topics from research, double-checked → Building your plan → 128 sessions to Sat 10 Apr → Fitting 128 pieces of work into your free time → 205 checks passed.
- ✗ **nothing unplaced**: 2 session(s) unplaced: Formula sheet and final review (40 min); Equity Investments past paper practice 6 (20 min)
- Strategy: 12 topics revised and practised in order, 4 mixed reviews, 8 timed Session 1s, light final day.
- Critics: realism 100: Every day is within what you usually sustain.; exam_prep 50: fewer than two spaced retrieval sessions; no light review on Sun 11 Apr; front_loading 100: Deadline work finishes with room to spare.; interleaving 100: Practice is mixed across subjects.; fragmentation 100: Sessions are a workable length.; neglect 100: Every active goal has time.
- Daily cap: 120 min
- Syllabus **CFA Level I** from model (researched), library new v1 CHECKED; identity CFA Institute / CFA Level I / CFA Program
- Papers: Session 1 135 min, 90 marks; Session 2 135 min, 90 marks
- Sources: https://acfr.aut.ac.nz/__data/assets/pdf_file/0006/1027545/CFA-Overview-AI-Python-David-Kandziora.pdf, https://careerlauncher.com/blogs/cfa/cfa-level-1-syllabus, https://pw.live/cfa/exams/cfa-syllabus, https://perlego.com/book/5037254/2026-cfa-program-curriculum-level-i-box-set-pdf?irclickid=SLPSnwQpZxyZTyBWnVzDUxlgUkr1GGSvCUJgVI0&utm_source=OCLC&utm_medium=affiliate&utm_campaign=Online%20Tracking%20Link&utm_content=ONLINE_TRACKING_LINK&discount=&irgwc=1&afsrc=1, https://duckduckgo.com/?q=CFA+Level+1+Curriculum&slpassthru=1&ia=web, https://annas-archive.gd/md5/e8a753089b7291296e0685d839e104b1

Topics:

1. **Ethical and Professional Standards** w3 d2: Apply the CFA Institute Code of Ethics and Standards of Professional Conduct to scenarios; Distinguish ethical duties to clients, employers and the investing public; Apply standards on integrity of capital markets and investment recommendations; Identify and manage conflicts of interest and disclosure requirements; Apply standards on performance presentation and GIPS fundamentals; Judge the appropriate conduct in practice-problem 'best action' cases
2. **Quantitative Methods: Time Value of Money** w2 d2: Compute present and future values for lump sums and annuities; Solve for interest rate, number of periods and payment amounts; Distinguish nominal, effective and stated annual rates; Use cash flow worksheets on the approved calculator
3. **Quantitative Methods: Statistics and Probability** w2 d2: Compute and interpret mean, median, variance and covariance; Apply probability rules, expected value and conditional probability; Describe normal, lognormal and binomial distributions; Apply sampling, central limit theorem and estimator properties; Conduct hypothesis tests and interpret p-values and test statistics; Interpret correlation, simple and multiple regression; Describe big data, machine learning and algorithmic trading basics
4. **Economics: Microeconomics** w2 d2: Analyse demand and supply shifts and market equilibrium; Compute and interpret price, income and cross elasticities; Compare market structures: perfect competition, monopoly, monopolistic competition, oligopoly; Explain consumer theory and indifference curves; Relate production, costs and profit maximisation
5. **Economics** w2 d2: Interpret national income accounting and GDP measures; Describe business cycle phases and economic indicators; Explain monetary and fiscal policy tools and their effects; Analyse inflation, unemployment and aggregate demand/supply; Explain exchange rate regimes and currency exchange determination; Describe international trade theories and trade restrictions; Summarise geopolitical and capital flow considerations for economies
6. **Financial Statement Analysis** w3 d2: Describe the roles and elements of the income statement, balance sheet and cash flow statement; Interpret financial reporting standards and the IFRS/US GAAP framework; Trace how transactions flow through the core financial statements; Apply general accounting principles such as accrual and revenue recognition
7. **Corporate Issuers** w2 d2: Evaluate capital budgeting projects using NPV, IRR and payback; Compute and interpret weighted average cost of capital; Describe capital structure theories and leverage effects; Explain corporate governance, ownership structures and stakeholder relations; Analyse business models, working capital management and short-term liquidity; Describe capital raising for public and private companies
8. **Portfolio Management** w3 d2: Explain the portfolio approach and investor characteristics; Compute portfolio expected return and risk; Interpret diversification, covariance and correlation; Describe risk aversion, mean-variance optimisation and efficient frontier; Explain risk management, portfolio planning and the investment policy statement
9. **Equity Investments** w3 d2: Describe market organisation, structure and trading mechanics; Compare order types, execution and market regulation basics; Explain market efficiency and its implications; Compare security market indices and their construction; Apply equity valuation concepts of intrinsic value, price multiples and present value models; Describe company analysis and industry analysis fundamentals
10. **Fixed Income** w2 d3: Describe fixed income instruments and issuers; Explain features of coupon bonds, floating rate and money market instruments; Compute bond prices, yields and yield measures; Explain yield curve shapes and term structure theories; Analyse interest rate risk, duration and convexity; Describe securitisation and fixed income asset classes
11. **Derivatives** w1 d2: Describe forwards, futures, options and swaps and their payoff profiles; Interpret futures pricing and forward pricing basics; Apply option pricing basics: intrinsic value, put-call parity, binomial and Black-Scholes intuition; Explain uses of derivatives: hedging, arbitrage and speculation
12. **Alternative Investments** w1 d1: Describe hedge funds, private equity, real estate, commodities and infrastructure; Explain fee structures and incentive alignment in alternative investments; Describe diversification, liquidity and valuation issues of alternatives

## gre-quant: GRE General Test: Quantitative Reasoning section

*Standardised admissions test, one section only.* Score **100**, planned as **exam**, due 2026-12-25, 20.5 s, 4 call(s). Stages: Reading your week → Reading your week · 0 commitments → Planning GRE General Test: Quantitative Reasoning section → Finding the syllabus → Researching this syllabus for the first time → Read 8 sources → Double-checking the topics → Checking 7 more official topics → Double-check made 7 corrections → 22 topics from research, double-checked → Building your plan → 59 sessions to Thu 24 Dec → Fitting 59 pieces of work into your free time → 241 checks passed.
- Strategy: 22 topics revised and practised in order, 7 mixed reviews, 3 timed papers (Quant Section 1), light final day. Time is tight, so sessions are short.
- Critics: realism 100: Every day is within what you usually sustain.; exam_prep 90: no light review on Thu 24 Dec; front_loading 100: Deadline work finishes with room to spare.; interleaving 100: Practice is mixed across subjects.; fragmentation 100: Sessions are a workable length.; neglect 100: Every active goal has time.
- Daily cap: 90 min
- Syllabus **GRE General Test, Quantitative Reasoning** from model (researched), library new v1 CHECKED; identity ETS / GRE / Quantitative Reasoning / Quantitative Reasoning section
- Papers: Quant Section 1 25 min, 13 marks; Quant Section 2 25 min, 15 marks
- Sources: https://careerlauncher.com/gre/exam-syllabus, https://msmunify.com/in/exams/gre/syllabus, https://studyark.in/blog/test-preparation/gre-exam-syllabus, https://gradding.com/blog/exams/gre-syllabus-and-pattern, https://keiko.study/blog/gre-syllabus, https://jamboreeindia.com/blog/latest-gre-syllabus-exam-pattern-sections-and-key-changes

Topics:

1. **Integers** w2 d1: Apply divisibility rules and factorisation of integers; Use prime factorisation to find factors and multiples; Find remainders and work with odd and even integers; Use the rules for the number of factors of an integer
2. **Fractions and decimals** w2 d1: Add, subtract, multiply and divide fractions; Convert between fractions, decimals and mixed numbers; Order fractions by comparing with a common denominator; Round decimals and perform operations on decimals
3. **Exponents and roots** w2 d2: Apply the rules of exponents to simplify expressions; Use negative and zero exponents correctly; Simplify square roots and estimate their values; Convert between radicals and fractional exponents
4. **Real numbers and the number line** w1 d1: Place numbers on the number line and compare absolute values; Work with properties of the real numbers and their signs; Apply the order of operations to evaluate expressions; Use estimation to check the size of a result
5. **Ratio and proportion** w2 d2: Solve part-to-part and part-to-whole ratio problems; Set up and solve direct proportions; Solve rate problems for work, speed and mixtures; Combine ratios into a single three-part ratio
6. **Percent** w2 d2: Convert between percents, fractions and decimals; Compute a percentage increase or decrease; Reverse a percent change to find the original amount; Solve successive percent change problems
7. **Algebraic expressions** w2 d2: Simplify algebraic expressions by combining like terms; Expand and factor common algebraic forms; Factor and use the notable products such as the difference of squares; Translate word problems into algebraic expressions
8. **Linear equations and inequalities** w3 d2: Solve single-variable linear equations; Solve linear inequalities and represent the solution on a number line; Solve systems of two linear equations in two variables; Solve word problems with linear models
9. **Quadratic equations** w2 d2: Solve quadratics by factoring; Use the quadratic formula to find roots; Use the discriminant to count real roots; Relate the roots of a quadratic to its graph
10. **Functions and their graphs** w2 d2: Interpret function notation and evaluate a function; Find the domain and range of a function; Read key features of a graph: intercepts, maxima and minima; Describe transformations of a graph
11. **Applications** w2 d2: Set up equations from word problems involving ratios, rates and mixtures; Translate verbal statements into algebraic models before solving; Solve rate and work problems with unit conversions; Check that the algebraic model answers the quantity actually asked for
12. **Coordinate geometry** w2 d2: Find the distance and midpoint between two points; Compute the slope of a line and write its equation; Identify parallel and perpendicular lines from their slopes; Solve problems involving lines and circles in the coordinate plane
13. **Lines and angles** w2 d1: Apply angle relationships for intersecting lines and transversals; Use the angle sum of a polygon; Apply properties of parallel and perpendicular lines
14. **Triangles** w3 d2: Use the angle sum and exterior angle theorems; Apply the isosceles triangle and side-angle relationships; Use the Pythagorean theorem and special right triangles; Find the area of a triangle and solve similar triangle problems; Compute the areas of 30-60-90 and 45-45-90 triangles
15. **Quadrilaterals and Polygons** w2 d2: Find the perimeter and area of rectangles, parallelograms and trapezoids; Use the properties of rhombuses and squares; Compute areas of regular polygons by splitting into triangles
16. **Circles** w2 d2: Find the circumference and area of a circle; Compute arc length and sector area; Use inscribed angle and tangent properties; Solve problems combining circles with other figures
17. **Three-dimensional figures** w2 d2: Compute the volume and surface area of rectangular solids; Compute the volume and surface area of cylinders; Find the diagonal of a rectangular solid; Solve problems on spheres and cones presented in the Math Review
18. **Presenting Data** w2 d1: Read and interpret bar, line and circle graphs; Read frequency distributions and histograms; Read and analyse tables of data; Identify misleading features of a graph
19. **Numerical methods for describing data** w2 d2: Compute the mean, median, mode and range of a data set; Interpret quartiles and the interquartile range; Use standard deviation to compare spread qualitatively; Describe how outliers affect mean and median
20. **Counting methods** w2 d2: Apply the multiplication principle to count arrangements; Compute permutations and combinations; Use counting to solve arrangement and selection problems; Handle counting problems with restrictions
21. **Probability** w2 d2: Compute the probability of simple and compound events; Use the complement rule and mutually exclusive events; Compute probabilities for independent events; Find conditional probabilities in simple cases
22. **Distributions and random variables** w2 d3: Interpret frequency distributions and their shape; Use properties of the normal distribution and its standard deviation bands; Compute the expected value of a simple random variable; Solve problems with uniform and binomial settings

## thinking-fast-slow: Read Thinking, Fast and Slow by Daniel Kahneman

*A long non-fiction book with many chapters.* Score **100**, planned as **reading**, due 2026-12-11, 5.2 s, 1 call(s). Stages: Reading your week → Reading your week · 0 commitments → Planning Read Thinking, Fast and Slow by Daniel Kahneman → Finding the chapters → 9 parts from the AI → Building your plan → 21 sessions to Thu 10 Dec → Fitting 21 pieces of work into your free time → 119 checks passed.
- Strategy: 9 parts at a steady pace, with written recaps.
- Critics: realism 100: Every day is within what you usually sustain.; exam_prep 100: No exams in view.; front_loading 100: Deadline work finishes with room to spare.; interleaving 100: Practice is mixed across subjects.; fragmentation 100: Sessions are a workable length.; neglect 100: Every active goal has time.
- Daily cap: 30 min
- Syllabus **Thinking, Fast and Slow (Kahneman)** from model

Topics:

1. **Introduction** w1 d1: Outline Kahneman's two-system picture of the mind; Locate where each part of the book argues its case
2. **Part 1: Two Systems** w2 d2: Distinguish System 1's fast automatic processing from System 2's effortful control; Explain cognitive ease, priming and the ideomotor effect; Show why System 2 is lazy and endorses System 1's suggestions; Recognise what attention can and cannot do at once
3. **Part 2: Heuristics and Biases** w3 d2: Apply the law of small numbers to research and everyday claims; Explain anchoring and the anchoring index; Describe the availability heuristic and affect heuristic; Recognise representativeness, base-rate neglect and conjunction fallacy; Identify less-is-more and causal-base confusions
4. **Part 3: Overconfidence** w3 d3: Explain the illusions of validity and skill in prediction; Describe formula vs. intuition in judging people; Give the conditions for intuitive expertise and why some professions fail them; Explain the outside view and how to take it; Critique cases where overconfidence is misplaced
5. **Part 4: Choices** w3 d3: Apply prospect theory to gains, losses and risk; Explain loss aversion, reference points and the endowment effect; Describe framing effects and mental accounting; Explain fairness, regret and the disposition effect
6. **Part 5: Two Selves** w2 d2: Explain duration neglect and the peak-end rule; Distinguish the experiencing self from the remembering self; Apply this to evaluations of well-being and policy
7. **Conclusions** w1 d2: Summarise the two-system thesis and its limits; Critique the rationality model of economics in light of the evidence
8. **Appendix A: Judgment under Uncertainty** w1 d3: Follow the original heuristics-and-biases argument; Relate it to the earlier development of the ideas
9. **Appendix B: Choices, Values, and Frames** w1 d3: Follow the original prospect theory formulation; Connect it to the value function and framing effects

## calc-midterm-tomorrow: Calculus 1 midterm tomorrow: limits and derivatives

*Edge case: a midterm tomorrow morning, Auto.* Score **100**, planned as **exam**, due 2026-11-14, 29.2 s, 4 call(s). Stages: Reading your week → Reading your week · 0 commitments → Planning Calculus 1 midterm tomorrow: limits and derivatives → Working out the plan → Looking up the syllabus → Read 6 sources → Double-checking the topics → Double-check made 2 corrections → 11 topics from the AI, checked against sources → Building your plan → 4 sessions to Fri 13 Nov → Fitting 4 pieces of work into your free time → 35 checks passed.
- Strategy: Quick revision of all 11 topics in 3 short sessions, then mixed practice on everything.
- Critics: realism 100: Every day is within what you usually sustain.; exam_prep 75: new learning in the final day; front_loading 40: 4 items finish on the due date with no room to run long.; interleaving 100: Practice is mixed across subjects.; fragmentation 100: Sessions are a workable length.; neglect 100: Every active goal has time.
- Syllabus **Calculus 1 Midterm: Limits and Derivatives** from model (researched)
- Papers: Midterm 90 min, 100 marks
- Sources: http://math.nyu.edu/~damron/Fall2007/Syllabus_new.pdf, https://ecourse2.ccu.edu.tw/pluginfile.php/417753/local_syllabus/attachment/14209/Syllabus-110Calculus.pdf, https://pearson.com/channels/calculus/study-guides/syllabus-overview-and-core-topics-for-calculus/video-lessons, https://commack.math.sunysb.edu/Courses/MAT131/201906/MAT131SumI2019.pdf, https://math.stonybrook.edu/~julia/MAT131S09/schedule.html, http://math.ucdavis.edu/~jcs/21A/21Asyll.html

Topics:

1. **The Limit of a Function** w2 d2: Estimate a limit from a table of values and from a graph; Distinguish one-sided limits and state when a two-sided limit exists; Recognise the cases in which a limit fails to exist; Use the precise epsilon-delta definition of a limit; Find limits involving infinity and identify horizontal asymptotes; Analyse limits of functions with a vertical asymptote
2. **Calculating Limits** w2 d2: Apply the limit laws to sums, products, quotients and powers; Evaluate limits by direct substitution; Evaluate limits of rational functions by factoring and cancelling; Rationalise numerators or denominators to remove a radical; Find limits using the factor and rationalisation techniques alongside the squeeze theorem
3. **Continuity** w1 d2: Test continuity at a point using the three-part definition; Classify discontinuities as removable, jump or infinite; Apply the Intermediate Value Theorem to show a root exists; Find constants that make a piecewise function continuous
4. **Derivatives and Rates of Change** w2 d2: Compute the slope of a tangent line as a limit of difference quotients; Write the equation of the tangent line at a point; Interpret the derivative as instantaneous velocity and as a rate of change; Use one-sided difference quotients to test differentiability at a point; Decide where a function is not differentiable (corner, cusp, vertical tangent, discontinuity)
5. **The Derivative as a Function** w2 d2: Compute the derivative function from the definition; Sketch the graph of the derivative from the graph of a function; Relate the sign of the derivative to where a function rises or falls; Find higher-order derivatives and interpret them
6. **Basic Differentiation Rules** w2 d1: Differentiate using the constant, power and constant-multiple rules; Apply the sum and difference rules; Differentiate polynomial and negative or rational power functions; Differentiate the natural exponential function
7. **Product and Quotient Rules** w2 d2: Apply the product rule to products of two or more factors; Apply the quotient rule correctly, including signs; Combine product and quotient rules in one expression; Use the rules to find tangent lines to rational functions
8. **Derivatives of Trigonometric Functions** w2 d2: Differentiate sine and cosine from their limits; Differentiate tangent, secant, cosecant and cotangent; Compute higher-order derivatives of sine and cosine; Differentiate expressions combining trig and polynomial terms
9. **The Chain Rule** w3 d3: Differentiate composite functions using the chain rule; Apply the chain rule with the power rule for nested powers; Differentiate compositions involving trigonometric functions; Differentiate multiple nested compositions
10. **Implicit Differentiation** w2 d3: Differentiate equations where y is defined implicitly; Apply the chain rule when differentiating terms in y; Solve for dy/dx in terms of x and y; Find tangent lines and higher derivatives from implicit equations
11. **Derivatives of Exponential** w2 d2: Differentiate the natural logarithm and general logarithmic functions; Differentiate exponential functions with base e and other bases; Use logarithmic differentiation for products and powers; Differentiate inverse trigonometric functions

## python-skill: Learn Python for data analysis with pandas

*Practical coding skill, longer sessions, no date.* Score **100**, planned as **skill**, due none, 5.7 s, 1 call(s). Stages: Reading your week → Reading your week · 0 commitments → Planning Learn Python for data analysis with pandas → Mapping the skill → 8 stages from the AI → Building your plan → 43 sessions to Mon 25 Jan → Fitting 43 pieces of work into your free time → 114 checks passed.
- Strategy: 8 stages, each with a lesson, practice and a milestone check.
- Critics: realism 100: Every day is within what you usually sustain.; exam_prep 100: No exams in view.; front_loading 60: 6 items finish on the due date with no room to run long.; interleaving 100: Practice is mixed across subjects.; fragmentation 100: Sessions are a workable length.; neglect 100: Every active goal has time.
- Syllabus **Python with pandas for data analysis** from model

Topics:

1. **Set up Python and run a script** w1 d1: Install Python and an editor such as VS Code or Jupyter; Create and activate a virtual environment; Install pandas and matplotlib with pip; Run a .py file and a notebook cell successfully; Read error messages and find the failing line
2. **Python basics for data work** w2 d1: Assign variables and use the main types: int, float, str, bool; Write lists and dictionaries, index and slice them; Use if/elif/else and for loops over a list; Define a function with parameters and a return value; Use f-strings to build readable output; Translate a familiar Excel formula into Python code
3. **Load and inspect a CSV** w2 d1: Import pandas with the standard import pandas as pd; Read a CSV with pd.read_csv, setting sep and encoding when needed; Inspect with head, tail, shape, columns and dtypes; Use info() and describe() to summarise a dataset; Spot wrong types such as numbers read as text
4. **Select and filter rows and columns** w3 d2: Select columns with brackets and rows with loc and iloc; Filter with boolean conditions and combine them using & and |; Use isin and between for range and list checks; Sort with sort_values and take the top rows; Compare this to Excel's filter and VLOOKUP habits
5. **Clean and reshape the data** w3 d2: Handle missing values with isna, dropna and fillna; Convert types with astype and to_datetime; parse dates; Rename columns and drop duplicates; Standardise messy text with str.strip, str.lower and str.replace; Use assign and the apply method to create derived columns; Detect outliers with describe or the IQR rule
6. **Group, combine and summarise** w3 d2: Aggregate with groupby and agg (sum, mean, count); Build pivot tables with pivot_table as in Excel; Merge and join two tables on a key column; Concatenate tables stacked with pd.concat; Chain steps with method chaining for readable code
7. **Chart the results** w2 d2: Plot a line chart of a series over time; Draw a bar chart of category totals; Make a histogram and scatter plot; Label axes, titles and legends clearly; Save the figure as a PNG at good resolution
8. **Work on your own real CSV end to end** w3 d3: Choose a dataset and state the question you want answered; Write a script that loads, cleans and summarises it; Produce two charts that answer your question; Export the cleaned table to a new CSV; Explain the findings in a short written summary
